    # actiontrack — Implementation Plan (Spec-Driven Development)
    
    | | |
    |---|---|
    | **Root spec** | [requirements.md](requirements.md). This is the source of truth, and module specs are derived from it |
    | **Status** | In progress. M0–M11 done |
    | **Date** | 2026-11-25 |
    
    ---
    
    ## 1. How we work
    
    Every module goes through the same steps, in this order:
    
    1. **Spec**: `modules/<M>/spec.md` is reviewed and marked *Approved*. Any change to the root spec is made there first.
    2. **Tests (red)**: every test case in `modules/<M>/tests.md` is written as a pytest test whose name contains its TC ID
       (e.g. `test_TC_M3_04_weekday_same_day_rolls_to_next_week`). They all fail.
    3. **Implement (green)**: work through `modules/<M>/tasks.md` in order, ticking tasks off as they pass.
    4. **Gate**: the module's Definition of Done (below) passes. Update the status board in §4.
    5. **Merge**: branch `feat/<m-id>-<name>` → PR → `main`. The PR description links the module spec and lists its TC IDs.
    
    ### Definition of Done (every module)
    - All TCs in the module's `tests.md` are implemented and passing.
    - `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` and `mypy --strict actiontrack` are clean.
    - Line coverage of the module's package is ≥ 90% for the pure modules (M1–M5, M9), and ≥ 80% for the others.
    - No public interface differs from the spec. If it had to, the spec was updated first (in the same PR).
    - Every REQ ID the module owns appears in at least one TC.
    
    ### Conventions
    - **IDs**: requirements `REQ-<AREA>-NN` (root spec §18) · modules `M0`–`M12` · tasks `T<m>.<n>` · test cases `TC-M<m>-NN`.
    - **Test locations**: `tests/unit/<package>/` for unit tests, `tests/integration/` for anything that touches SQLite, the file system or MCP,
      `tests/e2e/` for M12. Shared fixtures live in `tests/fixtures/notes/<scenario>/`.
    - **Markers**: `@pytest.mark.slow` for perf/e2e. The default `pytest` run excludes slow tests, and CI runs everything.
    - **Time**: tests never read the real clock. They use `FixedClock` (M0).
    
    ## 2. Modules
    
    | ID | Module | Package / files | Depends on | REQ IDs owned |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | [M0](modules/M0-foundation/spec.md) | Foundation | `pyproject.toml`, `cli.py` skeleton, `config.py`, `models.py`, `errors.py`, `clock.py`, `console.py`, `__main__.py` | — | CLI-01..07, CLI-09, CLI-10 |
    | [M1](modules/M1-note-loader/spec.md) | Note loader | `parsing/note_loader.py` | M0 | NOTE-01..07, GEN-03 |
    | [M2](modules/M2-people/spec.md) | People & owner resolution | `parsing/people.py` | M0 | PEOPLE-01..06 |
    | [M3](modules/M3-dates/spec.md) | Due-date extraction & resolution | `parsing/dates.py` | M0 | DATE-01..06 |
    | [M4](modules/M4-item-extraction/spec.md) | Action item extraction | `parsing/items.py` | M1, M2, M3 | ITEM-01..07 |
    | [M5](modules/M5-dedup/spec.md) | Dedup & item IDs | `dedup.py` | M0 (consumes M4 output types) | DEDUP-01..06, ID-01, ID-02 |
    | [M6](modules/M6-store/spec.md) | SQLite store | `store/db.py`, `store/repo.py` | M0 | STORE-01..06 |
    | [M7](modules/M7-ingest/spec.md) | `ingest` command | `services/ingest.py`, `cli.py` | M1–M6 | INGEST-01..09, GEN-02 |
    | [M8](modules/M8-list-done/spec.md) | `list` and `done` commands | `services/query.py`, `cli.py` | M3, M5, M6 | LIST-01..05, DONE-01..02 |
    | [M9](modules/M9-email-render/spec.md) | Follow-up email rendering | `email/render.py`, `templates/followup.txt.j2` | M0 | FU-04 |
    | [M10](modules/M10-gmail-mcp/spec.md) | Gmail MCP adapter | `email/gmail_mcp.py`, `email/outbox.py` | M0 | MCP-01..05, GEN-01 |
    | [M11](modules/M11-followup/spec.md) | `followup` command | `services/followup.py`, `cli.py` | M2, M6, M8, M9, M10 | FU-01..03, FU-05, FU-06 |
    | [M12](modules/M12-e2e-release/spec.md) | End-to-end, performance, release | `tests/e2e/`, `README.md`, CI | all | ACC-01..11, CLI-08 |
    
    Every REQ ID in root spec §18 is owned by exactly one module. Shared contracts (the dataclasses in `models.py`) are
    **defined in M0**. Later modules consume them and may add fields only by changing the M0 spec first.
    
    ## 3. Dependency graph and build order
    
    ```
                     ┌─> M1 Loader ─┐
                     ├─> M2 People ─┼─> M4 Items ─> M5 Dedup ─┐
    M0 Foundation ───┼─> M3 Dates ──┘                         ├─> M7 Ingest ─> M8 List/Done ─┐
                     ├─> M6 Store ────────────────────────────┘                              ├─> M12 E2E
                     ├─> M9 Render ─┐                                                         │
                     └─> M10 MCP ───┴─────────────> M11 Followup ────────────────────────────┘
    ```
    
    | Wave | Modules | Notes |
    |---|---|---|
    | 1 | M0 | Fixes the contracts (models, errors, config, Clock). Everything else waits on it |
    | 2 | M1, M2, M3, M6, M9, M10 | These don't depend on each other and can be done in any order or in parallel |
    | 3 | M4 | Needs M1–M3 |
    | 4 | M5 | Pure. Can start in wave 2 using hand-built `ParsedItem` fixtures |
    | 5 | M7 | First end-to-end path: notes → DB |
    | 6 | M8, then M11 | M11 reuses the owner query from M8 |
    | 7 | M12 | Needs the sample `notes/` folder (not yet in the repo) |

    ## 5. Spec gaps found while decomposing
    
    These came up while writing the module specs. Each has a **proposed resolution**, which the module specs already assume.
    Once a gap is confirmed, the root spec is updated and the gap is marked resolved here (✅). G1 and G2 were confirmed with M6
    (root spec §6, §8), G4 with M7 (root spec §9.1), G3 and G11 with M8 (root spec §9.2, §9.4),
    G7, G12, G13, G14 and G15 with M9 (root spec §9.4, §11),
    G8, G16–G22 with M10 (root spec §10.1),
    and Q5 and G23–G27 with M11 (root spec §9.4, §17, §19).
    
    | # | Gap | Proposed resolution | Affects |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | G1 ✅ | An attendee listed only by first name, with no email, has no person key. `people.email` is `NOT NULL` in §8 | Person key = `name:<firstname>` for such attendees. `people.email` becomes nullable. `followup` for them fails with "cannot derive email" | M2, M6, M11 |
    | G2 ✅ | Incremental ingest needs the parsed fields of every source to regroup items, and §8 `item_sources` stores only raw text | `item_sources` stores the parsed fields (owner_key, task, task_norm, due, checked, flags). PK becomes `(note_path, line_no)`, and `item_id` is reassigned on every regroup. Grouping always re-runs over **all** sources, so a change in config (threshold) applies everywhere | M5, M6, M7 |
    | G3 ✅ | Are `orphaned` items shown in `list` and included in `followup`? | Hidden from the default `list` (shown with `--flag orphaned`) and excluded from `followup` | M8, M11 |
    | G4 ✅ | A note file that was deleted from the folder | Its sources are removed and its items become `orphaned`. Only files under the scanned root are affected, so ingesting a different folder orphans nothing | M7 |
    | G5 | A single-token owner that is not in the attendees (`Vikram to …`) | `owner_key = NULL`, flags `owner_not_attendee` + `unassigned`, `owner_raw` kept. A full name gets a derived key | M2, M4 |
    | G6 | Meaning of `next Friday` (said on a Wednesday) | The occurrence in the **following calendar week** (Mon-start). `this Friday` / `Friday` = the next occurrence strictly after | M3 |
    | G7 ✅ | In the subject `N open, M overdue`, does N include the overdue items? | N = open items that are **not** overdue. Total = N + M | M9 |
    | G8 ✅ | The Gmail MCP server's `create_draft` argument names/shapes are unknown (Q6) | Configurable argument mapping (`arg_names`, `to_as_list`), validated against the tool's `inputSchema` | M10 |
    | G9 | `ASAP` as a due phrase | Recognised. `due_date` NULL, flag `unparsed_due`, `due_raw = "ASAP"` | M3 |
    | G10 | The same person appears with an explicit email in one note and a derived email in another | Known limitation: two keys. Future work: `people.yaml` aliases | M2 |
    | G11 ✅ | An owner written as a full name who is not an attendee (G5) gets a derived key, but only attendees are stored in `people`, so `--owner` / `followup` could not find them | `PeopleRepo.find` also matches item owners missing from `people` (name = `owner_display`, email = the key if it is an email). Read-only: no schema or ingest change | M6, M8, M11 |
    | G12 ✅ | The root spec names the template `followup.md.j2`, and the M9 spec names it plain-text `followup.txt.j2` | `templates/followup.txt.j2` (plain text). The root spec is renamed | M9 |
    | G13 ✅ | An `overdue` item passed to the renderer with no due date, or one not before `today` | `render_followup` raises `ValueError` (a caller bug, like both sections being empty) | M9, M11 |
    | G14 ✅ | A `sign_off` or task with trailing spaces breaks "no trailing whitespace" | Every rendered line is right-stripped, and the body ends with exactly one `\n` | M9 |
    | G15 ✅ | The M9 template `(due {{ due or "no due date" }})` reads "(due no due date)" for an undated item | Undated open items read `(no due date)`; dated ones `(due 2026-11-28)` | M9 |
    | G16 ✅ | The M10 spec used mcp v1 names (`FastMCP`, `streamablehttp_client`, camelCase result fields), but the installed SDK is mcp 2.x; `list_tools` is paginated | Target mcp 2.x (`mcp>=2.3,<3`): `MCPServer`, `streamable_http_client`, snake_case fields. Follow `next_cursor` until the draft tool is found | M10 |
    | G17 ✅ | The outbox file name's `<owner-key-slug>` rule was undefined | Lowercase; runs of chars outside `[a-z0-9._-]` → `-`; trim `-`/`.`; empty → `unknown`. Timestamp = `now` in its own timezone | M10, M11 |
    | G18 ✅ | The draft tool's schema may `require` arguments the client does not send | Fail before calling with `ExternalServiceError` naming them | M10 |
    | G19 ✅ | A Gmail API draft ID is usually not the compose ID, so the built `#drafts?compose=<id>` link may not open the draft | Use a `link`/`url` returned by the server when present; otherwise the built link | M10 |
    | G20 ✅ | The real server is the Gmail MCP connector (Q6), whose `create_draft` takes `to` as an array, but `to_as_list` defaulted to false | `McpConfig.to_as_list` defaults to `true`; other servers set it to false | M0, M10 |
    | G21 ✅ | The connector returns the draft link as `viewUrl`, which the G19 parser ignored | Link keys are `viewUrl`, `link`, `url`, in that order | M10 |
    | G22 ✅ | The connector is hosted and needs OAuth, and the adapter has no auth (REQ-MCP-05) | The adapter stays auth-free: `[gmail_mcp]` points at an already-authenticated endpoint (http `url` or stdio bridge). The endpoint is confirmed in M12's smoke test | M10, M12 |
    | G23 ✅ | Which short ID the follow-up email shows for each item | The same prefix `list` prints: `shortest_unique_prefixes` over all item IDs (≥ 6 chars) | M11 |
    | G24 ✅ | The MCP server creates a draft but returns neither an ID nor a link | Still a success: `drafts` row with `gmail_draft_id` NULL, stdout says the server returned no draft ID, exit 0 | M11 |
    | G25 ✅ | `[gmail_mcp]` is misconfigured (no `url`/`command`, non-allowlisted `draft_tool`) | The adapter's config error surfaces as is (exit 2, or 1 for `ForbiddenToolError`). No outbox file: nothing was attempted | M11 |
    | G26 ✅ | The draft is created but recording the `drafts` row fails (DB locked) | `FatalError` (exit 1) whose message names the draft's link or ID. No outbox, no second draft | M11 |
    | G27 ✅ | An item with an unparsed due phrase (`ASAP`) has no `due_date`, so the email reads `(no due date)` | Kept for now (no M9 change). Showing the raw phrase is future work (root spec §19) | M9, M11 |
    
    ## 6. Traceability check (run before each PR)
    
    ```
    # every REQ id in requirements.md §18 must appear in exactly one module spec and ≥1 tests.md
    grep -o 'REQ-[A-Z]*-[0-9]*' spec/requirements.md | sort -u > /tmp/req
    grep -rho 'REQ-[A-Z]*-[0-9]*' spec/modules/*/tests.md | sort -u > /tmp/tc
    comm -23 /tmp/req /tmp/tc      # → requirements with no test case
    ```
    (M12 adds this as `scripts/trace_check.py` and runs it in CI.)

  [result]:
    # M12 — End-to-End, Performance & Release
    
    | | |
    |---|---|
    | **Status** | Draft |
    | **Depends on** | All modules |
    | **Files** | `tests/e2e/`, `notes/` (sample data), `tests/e2e/expected.yaml`, `scripts/trace_check.py`, `README.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` |
    | **REQ IDs** | REQ-ACC-01 … 11, REQ-CLI-08 |
    
    ## 1. Purpose
    Prove the whole tool meets the acceptance criteria (root spec §16) on the provided sample notes. Check the CLI performance budget.
    Ship the README and CI that keep it that way.
    
    ## 2. Sample data
    - The provided `notes/` folder (30+ files, some long and messy, some deliberately malformed) is committed under `notes/`. **It isn't in
      the repo yet, and the user needs to provide it.**
    - `tests/e2e/expected.yaml` is written **by hand after reading the notes** (not generated by the tool):
      ```yaml
      skipped:            # file → reason
        notes/bad-yaml.md: invalid_frontmatter
      min_unique_items: 0           # filled in from manual review
      duplicates:                   # items known to repeat across notes
        - owner: priya.shah@coditas.com
          task_contains: revised quote
          min_sources: 3
      ambiguous:                    # lines with a shared first name
        - file: notes/x.md
          line: 14
      ```
    - The e2e tests run the CLI as a **subprocess** (`sys.executable -m actiontrack`) against a temp DB with `ACTIONTRACK_TODAY` fixed.
    
    ## 3. Acceptance mapping
    
    | REQ | Root §16 | Covered by |
    |---|---|---|
    | REQ-ACC-01 | Sample ingest has no crash, and every malformed file is listed | TC-M12-01 |
    | REQ-ACC-02 | A duplicate appears once, with a source count > 1 and the latest due date | TC-M12-02 |
    | REQ-ACC-03 | Two Priyas → `ambiguous_owner` | TC-M12-03 |
    | REQ-ACC-04 | No owner / no due listed, never overdue | TC-M12-04 |
    | REQ-ACC-05 | `by Friday` on Wed 2026-11-18 → 2026-11-20 | TC-M12-05 |
    | REQ-ACC-06 | `--status overdue` correct + highlighted | TC-M12-06 |
    | REQ-ACC-07 | `done` survives re-ingest | TC-M12-07 |
    | REQ-ACC-08 | followup → 1 draft, correct recipient, no send | TC-M12-08 |
    | REQ-ACC-09 | A second ingest changes nothing | TC-M12-09 |
    | REQ-ACC-10 | `list --json` is pure JSON on stdout | TC-M12-10 |
    | REQ-ACC-11 | Help has examples. No tracebacks on user errors | TC-M12-11 |
    
    ## 4. Performance (REQ-CLI-08)
    - `actiontrack --help`, `actiontrack list` (on the sample DB) and `actiontrack done <bad id>` each start in **< 300 ms** (median of 5
      subprocess runs after one warm-up, on CI's Linux runner). Marked `slow`.
    - Ingest of the sample folder: < 5 s (an informational budget that fails at 2× to catch regressions).
    
    ## 5. Release items
    - `README.md`: install (`pipx install .` / `uv tool install .`), configuration (with a Gmail MCP section), a quickstart, an example for each
      command, the exit code table, the "never sends" guarantee.
    - `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: matrix Python 3.11/3.12/3.13 × ubuntu/windows. Steps: `uv sync`, ruff, mypy, `pytest -m "not slow"`
      then `pytest -m slow` (ubuntu only), and `scripts/trace_check.py`.
    - `scripts/trace_check.py`: fails if any REQ ID in root §18 has no test case in `spec/modules/*/tests.md`, or is owned by more than one module.
    - A clean-venv install smoke test: build the wheel, install it into a new venv, `actiontrack --version`.
    - **Manual smoke test** (documented, not automated): with the real Gmail MCP server configured, `followup <me> ` creates a
      draft in your own Gmail. Confirm it shows up under Drafts and that nothing was sent.
    
    ## 6. Open questions
    The sample `notes/` folder must be provided. Q6 (the real MCP server) for the manual smoke test.

    ## 9. Commands
    
    Global options: `--db PATH`, `--config PATH`, `-v/--verbose` (repeatable), `-q/--quiet`, `--no-color`, `--debug`, `--version`, `-h/--help`.
    Extra command: `actiontrack config show` (§13.6).
    
    ### 9.1 `actiontrack ingest <folder>`
    - Recursively finds `*.md` files (non-recursive with `--no-recursive`).
    - For each file, if the `sha256` is unchanged since the last ingest it is skipped (D12). Otherwise the file is parsed, and its old
      `item_sources` rows are removed and re-inserted.
    - Items whose only sources were removed (e.g. the line was deleted from the note) are marked with the flag `orphaned`, not deleted.
    - A note that was ingested before and is no longer in the folder is removed, together with its sources, so its items become
      `orphaned` (G4). Only notes under the folder being ingested are affected (with `--no-recursive`, only its direct children),
      so ingesting a different folder orphans nothing. The report counts these as `removed`.
    - Never resets `status='done'` where `done_source='cli'`.
    - All writes for one run happen in a single transaction per file, so a failure on one file does not corrupt the others.
    - **Ingest report** (stdout, plus `--report-json PATH`):
      ```
      Scanned 34 files: 29 ingested, 2 unchanged, 3 skipped, 0 removed
      Items: 112 found → 87 unique (19 exact merges, 6 fuzzy merges)
      Flags: 5 unassigned, 2 ambiguous_owner, 4 no_due, 1 unparsed_due
      Skipped:
        notes/2026-10-03-standup.md   missing meeting date
        notes/broken.md                invalid YAML frontmatter (line 4)
        notes/retro-old.md             not valid UTF-8
      Line warnings:
        notes/2026-11-02-sync.md:41    malformed checkbox "-[ ] Rahul ..."
      ```
    - Exit code `0` even when some notes are skipped. `1` only on fatal errors (folder missing, DB not writable).
    
    ### 9.2 `actiontrack list`
    | Option | Meaning |
    |---|---|
    | `--owner TEXT` | Matches the person key, email, full name, or first name, of an attendee or of an owner that only appears on items (a full name not in the attendees, G11). An ambiguous first name prints the candidates and exits 2 |
    | `--status open\|done\|overdue` | `overdue` ⊂ `open` |
    | `--due-before DATE` | Any date `dateparser` understands, relative to today (e.g. `2026-12-01`, `next friday`) |
    | `--unassigned` | Only items with `owner_key IS NULL` |
    | `--flag NAME` | e.g. `ambiguous_owner`, `orphaned`. Orphaned items are hidden unless this is `--flag orphaned` (G3) |
    | `--json` | Machine-readable output |
    
    - The default view shows all open items, except orphaned ones (G3), sorted by due date (nulls last), then owner.
    - Table columns: `ID`, `Owner`, `Task`, `Due`, `Status`, `Src` (source count). **Overdue rows are shown in red**, and their status
      always reads `⚠ overdue`, so colour is never the only signal.
    
    ### 9.3 `actiontrack done <id>`
    - Resolves the ID prefix (§8.1) and sets `status='done'`, `done_source='cli'`, `done_at=now`.
    - Already done → prints a notice and exits 0. Unknown or ambiguous ID → exits 2.
    
    ### 9.4 `actiontrack followup <owner>`
    1. Resolves the owner (same rules as `--owner`). An ambiguous or unknown owner → error, no draft.
    2. Selects that owner's **open + overdue** items, excluding orphaned ones (G3) and `ambiguous_owner` items, which have no
       owner until resolved (Q5). If there are none → message, no draft.
    3. Resolves the recipient (D13):
       - the email from any attendees entry for this person, **or**
       - `firstname.lastname@coditas.com`, derived from the full name (lowercased, accents removed, spaces/hyphens in multi-part
         names joined with `.`, e.g. `Anna Maria Lopez` → `anna.maria.lopez@coditas.com`). *(Assumption about multi-part names; confirm.)*
       - Only a first name known → error `cannot derive email: no last name for "Priya"`.
       - The domain comes from config (`email.domain`, default `coditas.com`).
    4. Renders the email from a plain-text Jinja2 template (`templates/followup.txt.j2`):
       - Subject: `Follow-up: your open action items (N open, M overdue)`, where N counts the open items that are **not**
         overdue, so the total is N + M (G7). With no overdue items it is `(N open)`.
       - Body: greeting, an **Overdue** section and then an **Open** section, each item with its task, due date and source meeting
         (title + date), and a sign-off. Each item is tagged with the same short ID `list` prints (G23). An item with an
         unparsed due phrase (`ASAP`) reads `(no due date)` (G27).
    5. `--dry-run` prints the rendered email and does not call MCP.
    6. Otherwise it calls the Gmail MCP `create_draft` tool and prints the draft ID/link, then records a row in `drafts`.
       - A draft returned with neither an ID nor a link still counts as created: the row is recorded with no draft ID (G24).
       - A `[gmail_mcp]` config error (no `url`/`command`, a non-allowlisted `draft_tool`) fails before any MCP call with the
         config error's exit code and writes no outbox file (G25). Only a failed MCP call writes the outbox and exits 3.
       - If the draft was created but the `drafts` row cannot be written, the error (exit 1) names the draft's link or ID, so
         the user knows it exists (G26).
    7. **It never sends** (see §10.2).
    
    ## 10. Gmail MCP Integration (D4)
    
    ### 10.1 Client
    - Uses the official Python `mcp` SDK (`ClientSession`) over the transport set in config:
      ```toml
      [gmail_mcp]
      transport = "stdio"            # or "http"
      command   = "npx"              # stdio: server launch command
      args      = ["-y", "<gmail-mcp-server>"]
      url       = ""                 # http: server URL
      draft_tool = "create_draft"
      to_as_list = true              # the Gmail connector takes `to` as a list (G20)
      ```
    - The server is the **Gmail MCP connector** (Q6): `create_draft(to: [str], subject, body)` returning `id`, `threadId`, `viewUrl`.
    - Auth/OAuth is handled by the MCP server or a bridge in front of it; `[gmail_mcp]` points at an endpoint that is already
      authenticated. The CLI reads and stores no Google credentials (G22).
    - Targets the `mcp` 2.x SDK (`mcp>=2.3,<3`) (G16).
    - Before calling the tool, the client checks that `draft_tool` is in `list_tools()` (following pagination) and fails with a clear
      message if it is not. It also checks the tool's input schema: the mapped argument names must exist, and the tool must not
      require any argument the client does not send (G8, G18).
    - Timeout of 30 s. On a connection failure the rendered email is saved to `<user_data_dir>/outbox/<owner>-<ts>.eml` so the work
      is not lost, and the command exits 3. `<owner>` is a filename-safe slug of the owner key (G17).
    - The draft link is the one the server returns (`viewUrl`, `link` or `url`) when present, otherwise
      `https://mail.google.com/mail/#drafts?compose=<id>` (G19, G21).
    
    ### 10.2 No-send guarantee
    - The MCP adapter exposes **only** a `create_draft(to, subject, body)` method. No generic `call_tool` is available to the
      rest of the code.
    - A hard-coded **allowlist** `{"create_draft"}` is checked before every `call_tool`, and any other tool name raises
      `ForbiddenToolError`.
    - Tests assert that `send_message`, `reply` and `forward` are never invoked (§14).
    Exit codes: `0` ok, `1` fatal, `2` user input error (bad ID/owner/filter), `3` external (MCP) failure, `130` interrupted (Ctrl-C).
    
    ## 13. CLI Best Practices
    
    The tool follows widely accepted CLI conventions (see [clig.dev](https://clig.dev)). These are requirements, not suggestions.
    
    ### 13.1 Packaging & installation
    - Packaged with `pyproject.toml` (PEP 621) and a `[project.scripts] actiontrack = "actiontrack.cli:app"` entry point.
      `python -m actiontrack` also works (`__main__.py`).
    - Installable as an isolated tool: `pipx install .` / `uv tool install .`. Dependencies are locked (`uv.lock`) for reproducible builds.
    - Semantic versioning. `actiontrack --version` prints the version and exits 0.
    
    ### 13.2 Command design
    - Use a consistent `actiontrack <verb> [args] [--options]` pattern, with positional args only for the main object (`<folder>`, `<id>`, `<owner>`).
    - Every flag has a long form, and common ones also have a short form (`-v`, `-q`, `-h`). Flag names are the same in every command
      (`--owner` means the same thing everywhere).
    - The tool works fully non-interactively. It never blocks on a prompt, so it can be scripted and used in CI.
    - Commands are idempotent where possible (`ingest`, `done`), so re-running them is always safe.
    - The outward-facing action (`followup`) supports `--dry-run`, and prints exactly what was drafted and to whom.
    
    ### 13.3 Help & discoverability
    - `-h/--help` on the app and on every command. Each command's help has a one-line summary, a description, and **at least one example**.
    - If you run `actiontrack` with no command, it shows short usage plus the list of commands, not a stack trace.
    - Shell completion is available through Typer (`--install-completion` / `--show-completion`) for bash, zsh, fish and PowerShell.
    - A mistyped command suggests the closest match ("Did you mean `list`?").
    
    ### 13.4 Output
    - **stdout is for data, stderr is for everything else** (progress, warnings, errors, the ingest summary when `--json` is used).
      This keeps `actiontrack list --json | jq …` clean.
    - Output for people (rich tables, colour) only when stdout is a TTY. Plain output when piped.
    - Respect `NO_COLOR` and `--no-color`. Colour is never the only signal (overdue also gets the `⚠` marker).
    - `--json` output has a documented, stable schema with a `"schema_version"` field. Breaking changes bump it.
    - `-q/--quiet` suppresses everything except data and errors. `-v` / `-vv` increase the log level (INFO / DEBUG), and logs go to stderr.
    - Progress bar for `ingest` on stderr, only on a TTY.
    - On Windows, stdout/stderr are reconfigured to UTF-8 so names with accents and the `⚠` marker print correctly.
    
    ### 13.5 Errors
    - Errors are short, written for people, and say what to do next, e.g.
      `Error: ID "a3f" matches 2 items (a3f91c, a3f0d2). Use more characters.`
    - No Python traceback by default. `--debug` (or `ACTIONTRACK_DEBUG=1`) shows the full traceback.
    - Exit codes are fixed and documented (§12). Typer/Click usage errors use exit 2.
    - Ctrl-C rolls back the current DB transaction, prints `Interrupted`, and exits 130.
    
    ### 13.6 Configuration precedence
    `command-line flags` > `environment variables (ACTIONTRACK_*)` > `config file` > `built-in defaults`.
    - File locations come from `platformdirs` (OS-native, not hard-coded):
      - config: `user_config_dir("actiontrack")/config.toml` (e.g. `%APPDATA%\actiontrack\config.toml` on Windows, `~/.config/actiontrack/` on Linux)
      - data (DB, outbox): `user_data_dir("actiontrack")`
    - `actiontrack config show` prints the effective config and where each value came from (useful for debugging).
    - Secrets are never stored by the CLI (MCP server auth is handled by the server).
    
    ### 13.7 Performance
    - `list`, `done` and `--help` start in under 300 ms. Heavy imports (`dateparser`, `rapidfuzz`, `mcp`, `jinja2`) are
      **imported lazily** inside the commands that need them.
    - `ingest` streams files one at a time and does not load the whole folder into memory. The SQLite indexes used are
      `items(owner_key)`, `items(status, due_date)` and `item_sources(note_path)`.
    
    ### 13.8 Robustness & portability
    - `pathlib` everywhere. No shell-specific paths, and works on Windows, macOS and Linux.
    - Files are read as UTF-8, with BOM handling and `\r\n` line endings normalized.
    - SQLite uses WAL mode and a busy timeout, so a second instance waits instead of failing immediately.
    - Dates are timezone-aware for "today", using the local timezone (overridable with `ACTIONTRACK_TODAY` for tests and demos).
    
    ### 13.9 Code structure for the CLI layer
    - `cli.py` contains only argument parsing, output formatting and the mapping from exceptions to exit codes. All logic lives in `services/`.
    - Domain errors are typed exceptions (`UserInputError`, `ExternalServiceError`, `ForbiddenToolError`) that the CLI maps to exit codes in one place.
    - A `README.md` with installation steps, a quickstart, and an example for each command.
    
    ## 14. Testing & Quality (D16)
    
    - **Unit** (pytest): checkbox regex; owner extraction (connectors, `@mentions`, two Priyas); date resolution (weekday on the same
      weekday, EOW, month end, year rollover from a December meeting); normalization; exact/fuzzy dedup including the
      near-miss cases (`update doc` vs `update docs page`); email derivation; ID prefix resolution.
    - **Fixtures**: small hand-written notes for each edge case under `tests/fixtures/`.
    - **Integration**: `ingest` on the provided `notes/` sample folder (30+ files) with snapshot assertions on the report: count of
      skipped files, the expected malformed files listed, unique item count. Re-running ingest is idempotent (same DB state).
    - **CLI**: `typer.testing.CliRunner` for every command and exit code.
    - **MCP**: a fake in-process MCP server that exposes `create_draft`, `send_message`, `reply` and `forward`. Tests assert that only
      `create_draft` is called, and that a direct attempt to call another tool raises `ForbiddenToolError`.
    - **Static**: `ruff` (lint + format) and `mypy --strict` on the `actiontrack` package.
    - **Time**: "today" is injected through a `Clock` so overdue tests are deterministic.
    
    ## 15. Configuration
    
    `<user_config_dir>/config.toml` (see §13.6; all keys optional):
    ```toml
    [email]
    domain = "coditas.com"
    sign_off = "Thanks,\nMohit"
    
    [dedup]
    fuzzy_threshold = 88
    fuzzy_enabled = true
    
    [gmail_mcp]
    transport = "stdio"
    command = "..."
    args = []
    ```
    
    ## 16. Acceptance Criteria
    
    1. `ingest notes/` completes on the sample folder with no unhandled exception, and lists every deliberately malformed file as skipped with a reason.
    2. An item repeated across meetings appears **once** in `list`, with a source count > 1 and the latest due date.
    3. `- [ ] Priya to …` in a note with two Priyas is flagged `ambiguous_owner` and is not assigned to either one.
    4. Items with no owner/no due are listed (`--unassigned`) and never counted as overdue.
    5. `by Friday` in a note dated Wed 2026-11-18 is stored as `2026-11-20`.
    6. `list --status overdue` shows only open items whose due date is before today, highlighted.
    7. `done <prefix>` marks the item done, and re-running `ingest` does not reopen it.
    8. `followup "Priya Shah"` creates exactly one Gmail draft to `priya.shah@coditas.com` containing that person's open and overdue items, and **no send tool is ever invoked**.
    9. Running `ingest` twice in a row reports all files unchanged and makes no DB changes.
    10. `actiontrack list --json | python -m json.tool` succeeds: there are no warnings or progress output on stdout.
    11. Every command's `--help` includes an example, and no user error produces a Python traceback unless `--debug` is set.
    
    ## 17. Open Questions / Assumptions to Confirm
    
    | # | Item | Current assumption |
    |---|---|---|
    | Q1 | Weekday on the same day ("by Friday" said on a Friday) | The following week's Friday |
    | Q2 | A note re-lists a CLI-done item as `[ ]` | It stays done (future: `reopen <id>`) |
    | Q3 | Owner not in the attendees list | Kept, flagged `owner_not_attendee`. Email derived from the full name if available |
    | Q4 | Multi-part / hyphenated surnames in email derivation | Parts joined with `.`, accents removed |
    | Q5 ✅ | Does `followup` also include items with `ambiguous_owner` whose candidates include this person? | No, they are excluded until resolved (confirmed with M11) |
    | Q6 ✅ | Which Gmail MCP server is used (exact launch command / URL) | The Gmail MCP connector: `create_draft(to: [str], subject, body)`. The endpoint is configurable and already authenticated (G20–G22); confirmed in M12's smoke test |
    | Q7 | Multiple owners on one line ("Priya and Rahul to …") | The first owner is kept. Flag `multi_owner` |
    | Q8 | Non-ISO dates like `03/04/2026` | Read day-first (3 April). Configurable |

    """stdout/stderr consoles and logging (REQ-CLI-03, REQ-CLI-04, REQ-CLI-09).
    
    stdout carries data only; messages, warnings, progress and logs go to stderr.
    """
    
    from __future__ import annotations
    
    import logging
    import sys
    from collections.abc import Mapping
    from dataclasses import dataclass
    from typing import Literal
    
    from rich.console import Console
    
    LOGGER_NAME = "actiontrack"
    
    
    @dataclass
    class Output:
        out: Console  # stdout — data only
        err: Console  # stderr — messages, warnings, progress, logs
        quiet: bool
        verbosity: int  # 0 WARNING, 1 INFO, 2+ DEBUG
    
        def data(self, text: str) -> None:
            """Write plain data to stdout, unwrapped and without markup."""
            self.out.print(text, markup=False, highlight=False, soft_wrap=True)
    
        def info(self, message: str) -> None:
            if not self.quiet:
                self.err.print(message, markup=False, highlight=False, soft_wrap=True)
    
        def warn(self, message: str) -> None:
            if not self.quiet:
                self.err.print(f"Warning: {message}", markup=False, highlight=False, soft_wrap=True)
    
        def error(self, message: str) -> None:
            self.err.print(f"Error: {message}", markup=False, highlight=False, soft_wrap=True)
    
    
    def color_enabled(*, no_color: bool, env: Mapping[str, str]) -> bool:
        """Colour unless `--no-color` or a non-empty NO_COLOR. TTY detection is left to rich."""
        return not no_color and not env.get("NO_COLOR")
    
    
    def make_output(*, no_color: bool, quiet: bool, verbosity: int, env: Mapping[str, str]) -> Output:
        # No `file=`: rich resolves sys.stdout/sys.stderr at write time, and emits no
        # colour when the stream is not a terminal.
        color = color_enabled(no_color=no_color, env=env)
        color_system: Literal["auto"] | None = "auto" if color else None
        out = Console(color_system=color_system, no_color=not color, highlight=False)
        err = Console(stderr=True, color_system=color_system, no_color=not color, highlight=False)
        output = Output(out=out, err=err, quiet=quiet, verbosity=verbosity)
        _configure_logging(output)
        return output
    
    
    def configure_stdio_utf8() -> None:
        """Make stdout/stderr UTF-8 so accented names and the ⚠ marker print on Windows."""
        for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
            reconfigure = getattr(stream, "reconfigure", None)
            if reconfigure is not None:
                reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    
    
    class _ConsoleHandler(logging.Handler):
        def __init__(self, console: Console) -> None:
            super().__init__()
            self.console = console
    
        def emit(self, record: logging.LogRecord) -> None:
            try:
                self.console.print(self.format(record), markup=False, highlight=False, soft_wrap=True)
            except Exception:
                self.handleError(record)
    
    
    def _log_level(*, quiet: bool, verbosity: int) -> int:
        if quiet:
            return logging.ERROR
        if verbosity >= 2:
            return logging.DEBUG
        if verbosity == 1:
            return logging.INFO
        return logging.WARNING
    
    
    def _configure_logging(output: Output) -> None:
        logger = logging.getLogger(LOGGER_NAME)
        for handler in list(logger.handlers):
            if isinstance(handler, _ConsoleHandler):
                logger.removeHandler(handler)
        handler = _ConsoleHandler(output.err)
        handler.setFormatter(logging.Formatter("%(levelname)s %(name)s: %(message)s"))
        logger.addHandler(handler)
        logger.setLevel(_log_level(quiet=output.quiet, verbosity=output.verbosity))
        logger.propagate = False
    """actiontrack — action items from meeting notes."""
    
    __version__ = "0.1.0"
    from actiontrack.cli import main
    
    if __name__ == "__main__":
        main()
    """In-process fake Gmail MCP servers for the M10 tests (M10 tests.md), and a fake sink for M11.
    
    Every fake exposes `create_draft` next to the tools that must never be called (`send_message`,
    `reply`, `forward`) and records each call it receives. Every fake built is kept in `FAKES`, so a
    test-wide fixture can assert that no send tool was ever invoked (TC-M10-02).
    """
    
    from __future__ import annotations
    
    from collections.abc import AsyncIterator, Callable
    from contextlib import AbstractAsyncContextManager, asynccontextmanager
    from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    from typing import Any
    
    import anyio
    import mcp.types as types
    from mcp import ClientSession
    from mcp.client._memory import InMemoryTransport
    from mcp.server import Server
    
    from actiontrack.config import McpConfig
    from actiontrack.email.gmail_mcp import DraftResult
    
    FORBIDDEN = ("send_message", "reply", "forward")
    
    FAKES: list[FakeGmail] = []
    
    
    def schema(*names: str, required: tuple[str, ...] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """An object schema with string properties `names`, all required unless `required` is given."""
        return {
            "type": "object",
            "properties": {n: {"type": "string"} for n in names},
            "required": list(names if required is None else required),
        }
    
    
    def text_result(
        text: str, *, structured: Any = None, is_error: bool = False
    ) -> types.CallToolResult:
        return types.CallToolResult(
            content=[types.TextContent(type="text", text=text)],
            structured_content=structured,
            is_error=is_error,
        )
    
    
    @dataclass
    class FakeGmail:
        draft_schema: dict[str, Any] | None = field(
            default_factory=lambda: schema("to", "subject", "body")
        )
        result: types.CallToolResult = field(
            default_factory=lambda: text_result('{"id": "r-123"}', structured={"id": "r-123"})
        )
        delay: float = 0.0
        page_size: int | None = None
        calls: list[tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]] = field(default_factory=list)
    
        def __post_init__(self) -> None:

    """Effective configuration: flags > env (ACTIONTRACK_*) > TOML file > defaults (REQ-CLI-07)."""
    
    from __future__ import annotations
    
    import tomllib
    from collections.abc import Mapping
    from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    from pathlib import Path
    from types import MappingProxyType
    from typing import Any, Literal
    
    import platformdirs
    
    from actiontrack.errors import UserInputError
    
    APP_NAME = "actiontrack"
    
    Source = Literal["flag", "env", "file", "default"]
    DateOrder = Literal["DMY", "MDY"]
    
    ENV_KEYS: Mapping[str, str] = MappingProxyType(
        {
            "ACTIONTRACK_DB": "db_path",
            "ACTIONTRACK_EMAIL_DOMAIN": "email_domain",
            "ACTIONTRACK_FUZZY_THRESHOLD": "fuzzy_threshold",
            "ACTIONTRACK_DATE_ORDER": "date_order",
        }
    )
    
    # TOML (section, key) → Config field. A `None` section means the top level.
    _FILE_KEYS: Mapping[tuple[str | None, str], str] = MappingProxyType(
        {
            (None, "db_path"): "db_path",
            (None, "outbox_dir"): "outbox_dir",
            ("email", "domain"): "email_domain",
            ("email", "sign_off"): "sign_off",
            ("dedup", "fuzzy_threshold"): "fuzzy_threshold",
            ("dedup", "fuzzy_enabled"): "fuzzy_enabled",
            ("parsing", "date_order"): "date_order",
        }
    )
    
    _DEFAULT_ARG_NAMES: Mapping[str, str] = MappingProxyType(
        {"to": "to", "subject": "subject", "body": "body"}
    )
    
    _TRUE = ("1", "true", "yes", "on")
    _FALSE = ("0", "false", "no", "off")
    
    
    @dataclass(frozen=True)
    class McpConfig:
        transport: Literal["stdio", "http"] = "stdio"
        command: str | None = None
        args: tuple[str, ...] = ()
        url: str | None = None
        draft_tool: str = "create_draft"
        arg_names: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=lambda: _DEFAULT_ARG_NAMES)
        to_as_list: bool = True  # the Gmail connector takes `to` as a list (G20)
        timeout_s: float = 30.0
    
    
    @dataclass(frozen=True)
    class Config:
        db_path: Path
        outbox_dir: Path
        email_domain: str = "coditas.com"
        sign_off: str = "Thanks"
        fuzzy_enabled: bool = True
        fuzzy_threshold: int = 88  # 0..100
        date_order: DateOrder = "DMY"
        gmail_mcp: McpConfig = field(default_factory=McpConfig)
        sources: Mapping[str, Source] = field(default_factory=dict)  # where each key came from
    
    
    SETTING_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (
        "db_path",
        "outbox_dir",
        "email_domain",
        "sign_off",
        "fuzzy_enabled",
        "fuzzy_threshold",
        "date_order",
        "gmail_mcp",
    )
    
    
    def default_paths() -> tuple[Path, Path, Path]:
        """(config_file, db_path, outbox_dir) in the OS-native locations."""
        config_dir = Path(platformdirs.user_config_dir(APP_NAME, appauthor=False, roaming=True))
        data_dir = Path(platformdirs.user_data_dir(APP_NAME, appauthor=False))
        return config_dir / "config.toml", data_dir / "actiontrack.db", data_dir / "outbox"
    
    
    def load_config(
        *, flags: Mapping[str, Any], env: Mapping[str, str], config_path: Path | None
    ) -> Config:
        """Merge all layers. `flags` maps Config field names to values; `None` means "not given"."""
        default_file, default_db, default_outbox = default_paths()
        values: dict[str, Any] = {"db_path": default_db, "outbox_dir": default_outbox}
        sources: dict[str, Source] = dict.fromkeys(SETTING_KEYS, "default")
    
        if config_path is not None and not config_path.is_file():
            raise UserInputError(
                f"config file not found: {config_path}", hint="check the --config path"
            )
        file_path = config_path if config_path is not None else default_file
        if file_path.is_file():
            for key, value in _read_file(file_path).items():
                values[key] = value
                sources[key] = "file"
    
        for env_name, key in ENV_KEYS.items():
            raw = env.get(env_name)
            if raw:
                values[key] = _coerce(key, raw, f"environment variable {env_name}")
                sources[key] = "env"
    
        for key, value in flags.items():
            if value is None:
                continue
            if key not in SETTING_KEYS or key == "gmail_mcp":
                raise ValueError(f"unknown config flag {key!r}")
            values[key] = _coerce(key, value, "command-line flag")
            sources[key] = "flag"
    
        return Config(**values, sources=MappingProxyType(sources))
    
    
    def settings_view(config: Config) -> dict[str, tuple[Any, Source]]:
        """Each effective setting as (value, source), for `config show`."""
        return {key: (getattr(config, key), config.sources.get(key, "default")) for key in SETTING_KEYS}
    
    
    def _read_file(path: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
        where = f"config file {path}"
        try:
            with path.open("rb") as fh:
                data = tomllib.load(fh)
        except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as e:
            raise UserInputError(f"malformed TOML in {where}: {e}") from e
        except OSError as e:
            raise UserInputError(f"cannot read {where}: {e.strerror or e}") from e
    
        out: dict[str, Any] = {}
        for (section, name), key in _FILE_KEYS.items():
            table: Any = data if section is None else data.get(section, {})
            if not isinstance(table, dict):
                raise UserInputError(f"[{section}] must be a table in {where}")
            if name in table:
                label = name if section is None else f"{section}.{name}"
                out[key] = _coerce(key, table[name], f"{where} (key {label})")
        mcp = data.get("gmail_mcp")
        if mcp is not None:
            if not isinstance(mcp, dict):
                raise UserInputError(f"[gmail_mcp] must be a table in {where}")
            out["gmail_mcp"] = _parse_mcp(mcp, where)
        return out
    
    
    def _coerce(key: str, value: Any, where: str) -> Any:
        """Validate and convert one setting. String input (env, flags) is parsed."""
    
        def bad(expected: str) -> UserInputError:
            return UserInputError(f"invalid value {value!r} for {key} in {where}: expected {expected}")
    
        if key in ("db_path", "outbox_dir"):
            if not isinstance(value, str | Path) or str(value) == "":
                raise bad("a path")
            return Path(value).expanduser()
        if key == "email_domain":
            if not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip().lstrip("@"):
                raise bad("a domain such as coditas.com")
            return value.strip().lstrip("@").lower()
        if key == "sign_off":
            if not isinstance(value, str):
                raise bad("a string")
            return value
        if key == "fuzzy_enabled":
            if isinstance(value, bool):
                return value
            if isinstance(value, str) and value.strip().lower() in _TRUE + _FALSE:
                return value.strip().lower() in _TRUE
            raise bad("true or false")
        if key == "fuzzy_threshold":
            if isinstance(value, str):
                try:
                    value = int(value.strip())
                except ValueError:
                    raise bad("an integer between 0 and 100") from None
            if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int) or not 0 <= value <= 100:
                raise bad("an integer between 0 and 100")
            return value
        if key == "date_order":
            if not isinstance(value, str) or value.strip().upper() not in ("DMY", "MDY"):
                raise bad("DMY or MDY")
            return value.strip().upper()
        raise ValueError(f"unknown config key {key!r}")
    
    
    def _parse_mcp(table: Mapping[str, Any], where: str) -> McpConfig:
        def bad(name: str, expected: str) -> UserInputError:
            return UserInputError(
                f"invalid value {table[name]!r} for gmail_mcp.{name} in {where}: expected {expected}"
            )
    
        kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {}
        for name, value in table.items():
            if name == "transport":
                if value not in ("stdio", "http"):
                    raise bad(name, '"stdio" or "http"')
            elif name in ("command", "url", "draft_tool"):
                if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:
                    raise bad(name, "a non-empty string")
            elif name == "args":
                if not isinstance(value, list) or not all(isinstance(a, str) for a in value):
                    raise bad(name, "a list of strings")
                value = tuple(value)
            elif name == "arg_names":
                if not isinstance(value, dict) or not all(
                    isinstance(k, str) and isinstance(v, str) for k, v in value.items()
                ):
                    raise bad(name, "a table of strings")
                value = MappingProxyType({**_DEFAULT_ARG_NAMES, **value})
            elif name == "to_as_list":
                if not isinstance(value, bool):
                    raise bad(name, "true or false")
            elif name == "timeout_s":
                if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
                    raise bad(name, "a positive number")
                value = float(value)
            else:
                raise UserInputError(f"unknown key gmail_mcp.{name} in {where}")
            kwargs[name] = value
        return McpConfig(**kwargs)
    """Typer app: argument parsing, output formatting and exception → exit-code mapping only.
    
    Heavy dependencies (dateparser, rapidfuzz, mcp, jinja2) must only be imported inside command
    functions, never at module level here or in anything imported from here (REQ-CLI-08).
    """
    
    from __future__ import annotations
    
    import json
    import logging
    import os
    import sys
    import traceback
    from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence
    from dataclasses import dataclass
    from enum import StrEnum
    from pathlib import Path
    from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Annotated, Any
    
    import typer
    from rich.console import Console
    from rich.progress import BarColumn, MofNCompleteColumn, Progress, TextColumn
    from rich.table import Table
    from rich.text import Text
    
    # Typer vendors Click; its ClickException (usage errors, bad parameters) is not re-exported.
    from typer._click.exceptions import ClickException
    
    from actiontrack import __version__
    from actiontrack.clock import Clock, clock_from_env
    from actiontrack.config import Config, load_config, settings_view
    from actiontrack.console import Output, configure_stdio_utf8, make_output
    from actiontrack.errors import ActionTrackError, ExitCode, UserInputError
    from actiontrack.models import JSON_SCHEMA_VERSION, Flag, to_jsonable
    
    if TYPE_CHECKING:
        from actiontrack.services.query import ItemView
    
    PROG_NAME = "actiontrack"
    DEBUG_ENV = "ACTIONTRACK_DEBUG"
    CONTEXT_SETTINGS: dict[str, Any] = {"help_option_names": ["-h", "--help"]}
    NO_VALUE = "—"
    
    
    class ListStatus(StrEnum):
        OPEN = "open"
        DONE = "done"
        OVERDUE = "overdue"
    
    
    @dataclass
    class AppContext:
        config: Config
        output: Output
        clock: Clock
        debug: bool
    
    
    def _version_callback(value: bool) -> None:
        if value:
            typer.echo(f"{PROG_NAME} {__version__}")
            raise typer.Exit()
    
    
    def _app_callback(
        ctx: typer.Context,
        db: Annotated[
            Path | None, typer.Option("--db", help="SQLite database path.", show_default=False)
        ] = None,
        config: Annotated[
            Path | None, typer.Option("--config", help="Config file (TOML).", show_default=False)
        ] = None,
        verbose: Annotated[
            int, typer.Option("--verbose", "-v", count=True, help="More logging (-v INFO, -vv DEBUG).")
        ] = 0,
        quiet: Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--quiet", "-q", help="Only data and errors.")] = False,
        no_color: Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--no-color", help="Disable colour.")] = False,
        debug: Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--debug", help="Show full tracebacks on errors.")] = False,
        version: Annotated[
            bool,
            typer.Option(
                "--version",
                is_eager=True,
                callback=_version_callback,
                help="Show the version and exit.",
            ),
        ] = False,
    ) -> None:
        """Extract, track and follow up on action items from meeting notes."""
        if ctx.resilient_parsing:
            return
        if ctx.invoked_subcommand is None:
            typer.echo(ctx.get_help())
            raise typer.Exit()
        env = os.environ
        output = make_output(no_color=no_color, quiet=quiet, verbosity=verbose, env=env)
        ctx.obj = AppContext(
            config=load_config(flags={"db_path": db}, env=env, config_path=config),
            output=output,
            clock=clock_from_env(env),
            debug=debug or env.get(DEBUG_ENV) == "1",
        )
    
    
    def create_app() -> typer.Typer:
        """Build the app. Tests build their own instance to register throwaway commands."""
        app = typer.Typer(
            name=PROG_NAME,
            context_settings=CONTEXT_SETTINGS,
            invoke_without_command=True,
            pretty_exceptions_enable=False,
            add_completion=True,
        )
        app.callback()(_app_callback)
    
        @app.command()
        def ingest(
            ctx: typer.Context,
            folder: Annotated[Path, typer.Argument(help="Folder of meeting notes (*.md).")],
            recursive: Annotated[
                bool,
                typer.Option("--recursive/--no-recursive", help="Also read notes in sub-folders."),
            ] = True,
            no_fuzzy: Annotated[
                bool, typer.Option("--no-fuzzy", help="Merge exact duplicates only, for this run.")
            ] = False,
            report_json: Annotated[
                Path | None,
                typer.Option(
                    "--report-json",
                    metavar="PATH",
                    help="Also write the report as JSON to PATH.",
                    show_default=False,
                ),
            ] = None,
        ) -> None:
            """Ingest meeting notes and update the action item store.

        @app.command("list")
        def list_items(
            ctx: typer.Context,
            owner: Annotated[
                str | None,
                typer.Option(
                    "--owner", help="Person key, email, full or first name.", show_default=False
                ),
            ] = None,
            status: Annotated[
                ListStatus | None,
                typer.Option(
                    "--status",
                    help="open (the default; includes overdue), done or overdue.",
                    show_default=False,
                ),
            ] = None,
            due_before: Annotated[
                str | None,
                typer.Option(
                    "--due-before",
                    metavar="DATE",
                    help="Only items due before DATE (e.g. 2026-12-01, friday).",
                    show_default=False,
                ),
            ] = None,
            unassigned: Annotated[
                bool, typer.Option("--unassigned", help="Only items with no owner.")
            ] = False,
            flag: Annotated[
                Flag | None,
                typer.Option(
                    "--flag",
                    help="Only items with this flag. Orphaned items show only with --flag orphaned.",
                    show_default=False,
                ),
            ] = None,
            as_json: Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--json", help="Machine-readable output.")] = False,
        ) -> None:
            """List action items.
    
            By default shows open items (overdue included) sorted by due date, then owner.
    
            \b
            Examples:
              actiontrack list
              actiontrack list --owner priya --status overdue
              actiontrack list --due-before friday --unassigned
              actiontrack list --flag ambiguous_owner
              actiontrack list --json
            """
            app_ctx: AppContext = ctx.find_root().obj
            _list(
                app_ctx,
                owner=owner,
                status=None if status is None else status.value,
                due_before=due_before,
                unassigned=unassigned,
                flag=None if flag is None else flag.value,
                as_json=as_json,
            )
    
        @app.command()
        def done(
            ctx: typer.Context,
            item_id: Annotated[str, typer.Argument(metavar="ID", help="Item ID or unique prefix.")],
        ) -> None:
            """Mark an action item as done.
    
            ID may be any unique prefix (4+ characters) of the item ID shown by `list`.
    
            \b
            Examples:
              actiontrack done a3f91c
              actiontrack done a3f9
            """
            app_ctx: AppContext = ctx.find_root().obj
            _done(app_ctx, item_id)
    
        @app.command()
        def followup(
            ctx: typer.Context,
            owner: Annotated[str, typer.Argument(help="Person key, email, full or first name.")],
            dry_run: Annotated[
                bool,
                typer.Option("--dry-run", help="Print the email instead of creating a draft."),
            ] = False,
        ) -> None:
            """Draft a follow-up email to an owner about their open items.
    
            Creates a Gmail draft (it never sends) with the owner's overdue and open items.
            If the draft can't be created, the email is saved to the outbox folder (exit 3).
    
            \b
            Examples:
              actiontrack followup priya
              actiontrack followup "Rahul Mehta" --dry-run
            """
            app_ctx: AppContext = ctx.find_root().obj
            _followup(app_ctx, owner, dry_run=dry_run)
    
        config_app = typer.Typer(
            context_settings=CONTEXT_SETTINGS,
            help="Inspect the effective configuration.",
            invoke_without_command=True,
        )
        app.add_typer(config_app, name="config")
    
        @config_app.callback()
        def config_callback(ctx: typer.Context) -> None:
            """Inspect the effective configuration."""
            if ctx.invoked_subcommand is None and not ctx.resilient_parsing:
                typer.echo(ctx.get_help())
                raise typer.Exit()
    
        @config_app.command("show")
        def config_show(
            ctx: typer.Context,
            as_json: Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--json", help="Machine-readable output.")] = False,
        ) -> None:
            """Show the effective configuration and where each value came from.
    
            Sources, highest precedence first: flag, env (ACTIONTRACK_*), file, default.
    
            \b
            Examples:
              actiontrack config show
              actiontrack config show --json
            """
            app_ctx: AppContext = ctx.find_root().obj
            _config_show(app_ctx, as_json=as_json)
    
        return app
    
    
    def _config_show(app_ctx: AppContext, *, as_json: bool) -> None:
        view = settings_view(app_ctx.config)
        output = app_ctx.output
        if as_json:
            data: dict[str, Any] = {"schema_version": JSON_SCHEMA_VERSION}
            data.update(
                {
                    key: {"value": to_jsonable(value), "source": source}
                    for key, (value, source) in view.items()
                }
            )
            output.data(json.dumps(data, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
            return
        table = Table("Key", "Value", "Source")
        for key, (value, source) in view.items():
            shown = to_jsonable(value)
            table.add_row(key, shown if isinstance(shown, str) else json.dumps(shown), source)
        output.out.print(table)

        from actiontrack.store.repo import ItemRepo
    
        output = app_ctx.output
        check_folder(folder)  # before connect, so a mistyped folder doesn't create a database
        if app_ctx.debug:
            logging.getLogger(LOGGER_NAME).setLevel(logging.DEBUG)  # per-file tracebacks

        def tools(self) -> list[types.Tool]:

        def server(self) -> Server[Any]:
            async def list_tools(
                _ctx: Any, params: types.PaginatedRequestParams | None
            ) -> types.ListToolsResult:
                tools = self.tools
                if self.page_size is None:
                    return types.ListToolsResult(tools=tools)
                start = int(params.cursor) if params and params.cursor else 0
                end = start + self.page_size
                return types.ListToolsResult(
                    tools=tools[start:end], next_cursor=str(end) if end < len(tools) else None
                )
    
            async def call_tool(_ctx: Any, params: types.CallToolRequestParams) -> types.CallToolResult:
                self.calls.append((params.name, dict(params.arguments or {})))
                if self.delay:
                    await anyio.sleep(self.delay)
                return self.result
    
            return Server("fake-gmail", on_list_tools=list_tools, on_call_tool=call_tool)

    def create_initialization_options(
            self,
            notification_options: NotificationOptions | None = None,
            experimental_capabilities: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
            extensions: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
        ) -> InitializationOptions:
            """Create initialization options from this server instance.
    
            `extensions` advertises SEP-2133 extension support under
            `ServerCapabilities.extensions`; keys are extension identifiers (e.g.
            `io.modelcontextprotocol/ui`), values are per-extension settings.
            Defaults to `self.extensions`, which higher layers populate.
            """
            return InitializationOptions(
                server_name=self.name,
                server_version=self.version,
                title=self.title,
                description=self.description,
                capabilities=self.get_capabilities(
                    notification_options or NotificationOptions(),
                    experimental_capabilities,
                    extensions if extensions is not None else self.extensions,
        async def run(
            self,
            read_stream: ReadStream[SessionMessage | Exception],
            write_stream: WriteStream[SessionMessage],
            initialization_options: InitializationOptions,
            # When False, exceptions are returned as messages to the client.
            # When True, exceptions are raised, which will cause the server to shut down
            # but also make tracing exceptions much easier during testing and when using
            # in-process servers.
            raise_exceptions: bool = False,
        ) -> None:
            """Serve a single connection over the given streams until the read side closes.
    
            Thin wrapper over `serve_dual_era_loop`: enters the server lifespan,
            then drives the loop, serving the legacy handshake era and the modern
            per-request-envelope era (the client's first request decides which).
            Transports with their own lifespan owner (the streamable-HTTP manager)
            call `serve_loop` directly instead.

    """A fake Gmail MCP server over stdio, for the M12 e2e tests (T12.3).
    
    It exposes `create_draft` next to the tools that must never be called (`send_message`, `reply`,
    `forward`), reusing the M10 tool definitions, and appends every call it receives to the JSON
    file named by the first argument, so the test can read it after the CLI exits.
    
        python tests/e2e/fake_gmail_server.py <calls.json>
    """
    
    from __future__ import annotations
    
    import json
    import sys
    from pathlib import Path
    from typing import Any
    
    import anyio
    import mcp.types as types
    from mcp.server import Server
    from mcp.server.stdio import stdio_server
    
    sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]))
    from tests.integration.mcp_fakes import FORBIDDEN, schema, text_result  # noqa: E402
    
    DRAFT = {"id": "r-e2e-1", "threadId": "t-e2e-1", "viewUrl": "https://mail.example/drafts/r-e2e-1"}
    
    
    def build_server(log_path: Path) -> Server[Any]:
        tools = [types.Tool(name=n, input_schema=schema("to", "subject", "body")) for n in FORBIDDEN]
        tools.append(
            types.Tool(
                name="create_draft",
                input_schema={
                    "type": "object",
                    "properties": {
                        "to": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
                        "subject": {"type": "string"},
                        "body": {"type": "string"},
                    },
                    "required": ["to", "subject", "body"],
                },
            )
        )
    
        async def list_tools(
            _ctx: Any, _params: types.PaginatedRequestParams | None
        ) -> types.ListToolsResult:
            return types.ListToolsResult(tools=tools)
    
        async def call_tool(_ctx: Any, params: types.CallToolRequestParams) -> types.CallToolResult:
            calls = json.loads(log_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) if log_path.exists() else []
            calls.append({"name": params.name, "arguments": dict(params.arguments or {})})
            log_path.write_text(json.dumps(calls), encoding="utf-8")
            return text_result(json.dumps(DRAFT), structured=DRAFT)
    
        return Server("fake-gmail-stdio", on_list_tools=list_tools, on_call_tool=call_tool)
    
    
    async def serve(log_path: Path) -> None:
        server = build_server(log_path)
        async with stdio_server() as (read, write):
            await server.run(read, write, server.create_initialization_options())
    
    
    if __name__ == "__main__":
        anyio.run(serve, Path(sys.argv[1]))

        """Keep tests away from the real config/data dirs and from ACTIONTRACK_* / colour env vars."""
        import os
    
        for name in list(os.environ):

        yield home

    class Clock(Protocol):
        def today(self) -> date: ...
    
        def now(self) -> datetime:
            """Timezone-aware, in the local timezone."""
            ...
    
    
    class SystemClock:
        def today(self) -> date:
            return self.now().date()
    
        def now(self) -> datetime:
            return datetime.now().astimezone()
    
    
    class FixedClock:
        """A clock frozen at a given day. `now` defaults to the start of that day, local time."""
    
        def __init__(self, today: date, now: datetime | None = None) -> None:
            self._today = today
            self._now = now if now is not None else datetime.combine(today, time()).astimezone()
    
        def today(self) -> date:
            return self._today

    ## 3. Processing rules (in order)
    
    | Step | Rule | On failure |
    |---|---|---|
    | 1. Read | `path.read_bytes()`. The file is opened read-only and never written (REQ-GEN-03) | `OSError` → `READ_ERROR` (detail = the error message) |
    | 2. Empty | 0 bytes, or only whitespace after decoding | `EMPTY_FILE` |
    | 3. Binary | A NUL byte in the first 8 KiB | `BINARY_FILE` |
    | 4. Decode | `utf-8-sig` (BOM removed) | `UnicodeDecodeError` → `INVALID_ENCODING` (detail = byte offset) |
    | 5. Normalize | `\r\n` and `\r` → `\n` | — |
    | 6. Hash | `sha256` of the **raw bytes** (before decode) | — |
    | 7. Frontmatter | If line 1 is exactly `---` (trailing whitespace allowed): find the next line that is `---` or `...`. YAML-parse the block with `yaml.safe_load` | No closing fence, YAML error, or a result that isn't a mapping → `INVALID_FRONTMATTER`, `line_no` = the YAML error line (offset into the file) |
    | 8. Body | All lines after the frontmatter (or every line if there is none), keeping the **original file line numbers** | — |
    | 9. Meeting date | The first one found from: (a) frontmatter `date` (a YAML date or a string); (b) a body line matching `^\s*\**date\**\s*:\s*(.+)$` (case-insensitive) before the first checkbox line; (c) the first H1 (`# …`) containing a date substring | No candidate → `MISSING_DATE`. Candidate but `parse_meeting_date` gives None → `UNPARSEABLE_DATE` (detail = the raw value) |
    | 10. Title | Frontmatter `title`, else the H1 text with the date and separators (`—`, `-`, `:`, `|`) removed, else None | — |
    | 11. Attendees | The first one found from: (a) frontmatter `attendees` / `participants` (a list, or a string split on `,`/`;`); (b) a body line `Attendees:` / `Participants:` (case-insensitive, optional bold) with a `,`/`;` list; (c) a heading `## Attendees` / `## Participants` followed by a bullet list (ends at the next heading or a blank line after the bullets). Entries are trimmed, empties dropped, order kept | No non-empty entry → `MISSING_ATTENDEES` |

    _report(f"Error: {e.message}", debug=debug)
            if e.hint:
                _err(f"Hint: {e.hint}")

            _err(f"Error: {e.format_message()}")

            _err("Interrupted")

            _report(

        if rv == ExitCode.INTERRUPTED:
            _err("Interrupted")
        return rv if isinstance(rv, int) else int(ExitCode.OK)

        configure_stdio_utf8()

    def _report(message: str, *, debug: bool) -> None:
        if debug:

    def _err(message: str) -> None:
        sys.stderr.write(message + "\n")

        def start(self, total: int) -> None:
            self._progress.update(self._task, total=total)
            self._progress.start()
    
        def advance(self, path: Path) -> None:
            self._progress.update(self._task, advance=1, name=path.name)

    app = create_app()
    
    
    def run(
        argv: Sequence[str] | None = None,
        *,
        app_: typer.Typer | None = None,
        env: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
    ) -> int:
        """Run the CLI and return the exit code. All error → exit-code mapping lives here."""
        args = list(sys.argv[1:] if argv is None else argv)
        env = os.environ if env is None else env
        debug = "--debug" in args or env.get(DEBUG_ENV) == "1"
        command = typer.main.get_command(app_ if app_ is not None else app)
        try:
            rv = command.main(args=args, prog_name=PROG_NAME, standalone_mode=False)
        except ActionTrackError as e:
            _report(f"Error: {e.message}", debug=debug)

  [result]:
    err: rich.console.Console     # stderr — messages, warnings, progress, logs
        quiet: bool
        verbosity: int                # 0 WARNING, 1 INFO, 2+ DEBUG
    
    def make_output(*, no_color: bool, quiet: bool, verbosity: int, env: Mapping[str, str]) -> Output
    def configure_stdio_utf8() -> None     # sys.stdout/stderr.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    ```
    - Colour is used only if the stream is a TTY **and** `NO_COLOR` is not set **and** `--no-color` is not passed.
    - `logging` is configured to write to `Output.err` at the level set by verbosity. `-q` forces ERROR.
    - The helpers `info()`, `warn()` and `error()` are methods on `Output` and all write to stderr. `warn` and `info` are
      suppressed by `-q`. `Output.data(text)` writes unwrapped plain text to stdout (for JSON).
    
    ## 7. CLI skeleton (REQ-CLI-02, REQ-CLI-05, REQ-CLI-06) — `cli.py`
    
    - A `typer.Typer` app with a global callback holding the options: `--db`, `--config`, `-v/--verbose` (count), `-q/--quiet`,
      `--no-color`, `--debug`, `--version` (eager), `-h/--help`.
    - The callback builds an `AppContext(config, output, clock, debug)` and stores it in `ctx.obj`.
    - With no command: prints usage and the command list to stdout, exit 0.
    - Commands `ingest`, `list`, `done` and `followup` are **registered as stubs** that raise `FatalError("not implemented")`.
      M7, M8 and M11 replace them. `config show` is implemented here.
    - Every command's help text has a one-line summary, a description and an `Examples:` block.
    - Typo suggestion: an unknown command → `Error: No such command 'lst'. Did you mean 'list'?` (difflib, cutoff 0.6), exit 2.
    - `create_app() -> Typer` builds the app (tests build their own to register throwaway commands); `app = create_app()`.
    - `run(argv=None, *, app_=None, env=None) -> int` holds the error boundary below and returns the exit code;
      `main()` = `configure_stdio_utf8()` + `sys.exit(run())`. The boundary:
      - `ActionTrackError` → `Error: <message>` (+ `Hint: <hint>`) on stderr, `exit(e.exit_code)`.
      - `KeyboardInterrupt` → `Interrupted` on stderr, exit 130.
      - Any other `Exception` → `Error: internal error (<TypeName>). Re-run with --debug for details.`, exit 1.
      - `--debug` / `ACTIONTRACK_DEBUG=1` → also print the full traceback to stderr.
    - `config show` prints each effective key, its value and its source (`flag/env/file/default`). `--json` is supported.
    - **Lazy imports**: `cli.py` and everything it imports at module level must not import `dateparser`, `rapidfuzz`, `mcp` or `jinja2`

    sub(C, "import logging\nimport sys\n", "import logging\nimport os\nimport sys\n")
    sub(C, '''LOGGER_NAME = "actiontrack"
    ''', '''LOGGER_NAME = "actiontrack"
    
    
    class StdoutClosed(Exception):  # noqa: N818 — a signal, not an error
        """The reader of stdout went away (`actiontrack list | head -3`) (G36)."""
    
    
    class _Console(Console):
        def on_broken_pipe(self) -> None:
            # rich's default exits 1; the CLI boundary turns this into a quiet exit 0 instead.
            self.quiet = True
            raise StdoutClosed
    ''')
    sub(C, '''    color_system: Literal["auto"] | None = "auto" if color else None
        out = Console(color_system=color_system, no_color=not color, highlight=False)
        err = Console(stderr=True, color_system=color_system, no_color=not color, highlight=False)
    ''', '''    color_system: Literal["auto"] | None = "auto" if color else None
        # Forced colour must be ANSI: legacy Win32 console calls never reach a pipe (G35).
        legacy_windows = False if env.get("FORCE_COLOR") else None
        out = _Console(
            color_system=color_system,
            no_color=not color,
            highlight=False,
            legacy_windows=legacy_windows,
        )
        err = _Console(
            stderr=True,
            color_system=color_system,
            no_color=not color,
            highlight=False,
            legacy_windows=legacy_windows,
        )
    ''')
    sub(C, '''def configure_stdio_utf8() -> None:''', '''def stdout_closed(error: BaseException) -> bool:
        """Whether `error` means the reader of stdout has gone (G36)."""
        if isinstance(error, StdoutClosed | BrokenPipeError):
            return True
        if not isinstance(error, OSError):
            return False
        try:  # on Windows a closed pipe raises OSError(EINVAL); the pending write fails again
            sys.stdout.flush()
        except OSError:
            return True
        return False
    
    
    def silence_stdout() -> None:
        """Point stdout at the null device, so the interpreter's final flush can't fail."""
        try:
            devnull = os.open(os.devnull, os.O_WRONLY)
            os.dup2(devnull, sys.stdout.fileno())
        except (OSError, ValueError):  # no real file descriptor (e.g. under test capture)
            pass
    
    
    def configure_stdio_utf8() -> None:''')
    
    L = "actiontrack/cli.py"
    sub(L, "from actiontrack.console import Output, configure_stdio_utf8, make_output",
        "from actiontrack.console import (\n    Output,\n    configure_stdio_utf8,\n    make_output,\n    silence_stdout,\n    stdout_closed,\n)")
    sub(L, '''    except Exception as e:
            _report(
                f"Error: internal error''', '''    except Exception as e:
            if stdout_closed(e):
                silence_stdout()
                return int(ExitCode.OK)
            _report(
                f"Error: internal error''')

    def finish(self) -> None:
            self._progress.stop()
    
    
    app = create_app()
    
    
    def run(
        argv: Sequence[str] | None = None,
        *,
        app_: typer.Typer | None = None,
        env: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
    ) -> int:
        """Run the CLI and return the exit code. All error → exit-code mapping lives here."""
        args = list(sys.argv[1:] if argv is None else argv)
        env = os.environ if env is None else env
        debug = "--debug" in args or env.get(DEBUG_ENV) == "1"
        command = typer.main.get_command(app_ if app_ is not None else app)
        try:
            rv = command.main(args=args, prog_name=PROG_NAME, standalone_mode=False)
        except ActionTrackError as e:
            _report(f"Error: {e.message}", debug=debug)
            if e.hint:
                _err(f"Hint: {e.hint}")
            return int(e.exit_code)
        except ClickException as e:
            _err(f"Error: {e.format_message()}")
            _err(f"Run '{PROG_NAME} --help' for usage.")
            return e.exit_code
        except (KeyboardInterrupt, typer.Abort):
            _err("Interrupted")
            return int(ExitCode.INTERRUPTED)
        except Exception as e:
            if stdout_closed(e):
                silence_stdout()
                return int(ExitCode.OK)
            _report(
                f"Error: internal error ({type(e).__name__}). Re-run with --debug for details.",
                debug=debug,
            )
            return int(ExitCode.FATAL)
